{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Fgh2mPC6fVdSHbtLwAKtHc","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→```markdown\n     2→# CommerceSphere Enterprise Suite – Architecture Overview\n     3→*(Project codename: `web_ecommerce`)*\n     4→\n     5→> Version: **v1.7.0**  \n     6→> Last updated: **2024-06-03**  \n     7→> Maintainer: **architecture@commercesphere.io**\n     8→\n     9→---\n    10→\n    11→## 1. Executive Summary\n    12→CommerceSphere Enterprise Suite (CES) is an **all-in-one B2B commerce platform** delivered as a single JVM-based deployable. The monolithic approach intentionally contrasts with micro-service sprawl, simplifying governance while retaining **strong modular boundaries** enforced through *package-by-component* architecture, Spring Boot’s hierarchical contexts, and a shared JPA domain model.\n    13→\n    14→---\n    15→\n    16→## 2. High-Level Logical Architecture\n    17→\n    18→```mermaid\n    19→graph TD\n    20→    subgraph Client Tier\n    21→        A1(Browser) -->|HTTPS| B1[API Gateway<br>Spring MVC]\n    22→        A2(Mobile/Tablet) -->|HTTPS| B1\n    23→    end\n    24→    subgraph Application JVM\n    25→        B1 --> C1[Auth Middleware<br>Spring Security]\n    26→        C1 --> C2[REST Controllers]\n    27→        C2 --> C3[Service Layer<br/>Transactional Façades]\n    28→        C3 --> C4[Domain Model<br/>JPA Entities]\n    29→        C3 --> C5[Payment Orchestrator]\n    30→        C3 --> C6[Pricing & Contracts Engine]\n    31→        C3 --> C7[Approval Workflow Engine]\n    32→    end\n    33→    C4 --> D1[(PostgreSQL)]\n    34→    C5 --> D2[[External PSPs]]\n    35→    C7 --> D3[(Redis Queue)]\n    36→```\n    37→\n    38→* All layers run **in-process** to avoid network overhead.  \n    39→* Public and admin UIs use the same REST resources safeguarded by **role-based ACLs**.  \n    40→* **Spring Transactional** boundaries encapsulate service operations, guaranteeing atomic DB writes and eventual consistency with PSPs via SAGA compensations.\n    41→\n    42→---\n    43→\n    44→## 3. Component Decomposition\n    45→\n    46→| Module                     | Maven GAV (`groupId:artifactId`)  | Responsibility                                                  |\n    47→|----------------------------|-----------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------|\n    48→| `ces-core`                | `io.commercesphere:ces-core`       | Shared domain model, enumerations, and value objects            |\n    49→| `ces-auth`                | `io.commercesphere:ces-auth`       | JWT/OAuth2 flows, SAML integration, MFA                         |\n    50→| `ces-payment`             | `io.commercesphere:ces-payment`    | PSP adapters (Adyen, Stripe, Authorize.Net), PCI token vault    |\n    51→| `ces-pricing`             | `io.commercesphere:ces-pricing`    | Tiered/contract pricing, promotions, taxes                      |\n    52→| `ces-workflow`            | `io.commercesphere:ces-workflow`   | BPMN-based approval and quote workflow engine                   |\n    53→| `ces-admin-ui`            | `io.commercesphere:ces-admin-ui`   | Thymeleaf SPA for ops, finance, and audit                       |\n    54→| `ces-public-ui`           | `io.commercesphere:ces-public-ui`  | Storefront, search, cart, checkout                              |\n    55→\n    56→> NOTE: Even in a monolith, *module jars* enforce clear **compile-time boundaries** to combat “big-ball-of-mud” risks.\n    57→\n    58→---\n    59→\n    60→## 4. Request Lifecycle Walkthrough (`Place Order`)\n    61→\n    62→1. **Browser** posts `/api/v1/orders` with `Authorization: Bearer <JWT>`.\n    63→2. Spring Security’s `JwtAuthenticationFilter` populates `SecurityContext`.\n    64→3. `OrderController#createOrder` receives DTO, delegates to `OrderService`.\n    65→4. `OrderService`:\n    66→   a. Locks inventory via `InventoryService.lock(sku, qty)`.  \n    67→   b. Computes contract price via `PricingFacade#getPrice(...)`.  \n    68→   c. Persists `Order` aggregate (`@Transactional`).  \n    69→   d. Hands off payment to `PaymentFacade` (asynchronous SAGA start).  \n    70→5. `PaymentFacade` pushes message to *Redis Stream* for background capture.  \n    71→6. Controller returns **202 Accepted** with tracking URL `/orders/{id}`.  \n    72→7. Background worker finalizes payment, triggers `OrderPaidEvent`.  \n    73→8. Event listener marks order `PAID`, publishes WebSocket notification.\n    74→\n    75→Sequence Diagram *(simplified)*:\n    76→\n    77→```mermaid\n    78→sequenceDiagram\n    79→    participant Client\n    80→    participant Controller\n    81→    participant Service\n    82→    participant Payment\n    83→    participant DB\n    84→\n    85→    Client->>Controller: POST /orders\n    86→    Controller->>Service: createOrder(dto)\n    87→    Service->>DB: INSERT order\n    88→    Service->>Payment: initiateCapture(orderId)\n    89→    Payment-->>DB: UPDATE order status\n    90→    Controller-->>Client: 202 Accepted\n    91→```\n    92→\n    93→---\n    94→\n    95→## 5. Data Model Highlights\n    96→\n    97→• **Order Aggregate**  \n    98→```\n    99→Order 1─* OrderLine\n   100→Order 1─1 ShippingAddress\n   101→Order 1─1 Invoice\n   102→```\n   103→\n   104→• **Price Rule Engine** uses *rule chains* persisted as JSON blobs enabling **hot-swapping** without redeploy.\n   105→\n   106→---\n   107→\n   108→## 6. Technology & Frameworks\n   109→\n   110→| Concern            | Technology                         | Justification                                          |\n   111→|--------------------|------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------|\n   112→| DI / Bootstrapping | Spring Boot 3.x                    | Mature ecosystem, Actuator, Observability              |\n   113→| ORM                | Hibernate 6 + Jakarta Persistence  | Rich mapping, batching                                 |\n   114→| DB                 | PostgreSQL 15                      | ACID, native JSONB                                     |\n   115→| Caching            | Redis 7 (Cluster)                  | Low-latency catalog & session caching                  |\n   116→| Messaging          | Redis Streams                      | Lightweight, in-JVM simplicity                         |\n   117→| Build              | Maven Centralized BOM             | Reproducible builds, SBOM generation                   |\n   118→| CI/CD              | GitHub Actions → Artifactory → K8s | Policy-driven pipelines, image scanning (Trivy)        |\n   119→| Observability      | OpenTelemetry, Grafana, ELK        | Distributed tracing, log correlation                   |\n   120→\n   121→---\n   122→\n   123→## 7. Deployment Topology\n   124→\n   125→```mermaid\n   126→flowchart LR\n   127→    subgraph k8s-cluster\n   128→        app[ces-app:war<br/>Tomcat 10]:::pod\n   129→        redis[(Redis Cluster)]:::svc\n   130→        db[(Postgres HA)]:::svc\n   131→        ingress[[NGINX Ingress]]:::svc\n   132→    end\n   133→    classDef pod fill:#c6e5ff,stroke:#036,border-width:1px;\n   134→    classDef svc fill:#fde3a7,stroke:#b36,stroke-width:1px;\n   135→\n   136→    ingress --> app\n   137→    app --> redis\n   138→    app --> db\n   139→```\n   140→\n   141→* Stateless app pods enable **horizontal scaling**.  \n   142→* Persistent data lives solely in PostgreSQL; Redis is a cache/queue and can be rebuilt.\n   143→\n   144→---\n   145→\n   146→## 8. Error Handling & Resilience\n   147→\n   148→1. **Controller Advice** (`@RestControllerAdvice`) translates exceptions to RFC-7807 *Problem Details* JSON.  \n   149→2. **Retry Template** wraps outbound PSP calls (`maxAttempts=3`, exp back-off).  \n   150→3. Non-recoverable errors push to **Dead Letter Stream** for ops triage.  \n   151→4. Circuit Breakers (`Resilience4j`) guard fragile integrations.\n   152→\n   153→---\n   154→\n   155→## 9. Security & Compliance\n   156→\n   157→| Area             | Initiative                                        |\n   158→|------------------|---------------------------------------------------|\n   159→| Authentication   | OAuth2 + SAML SLO, enforced MFA (TOTP)            |\n   160→| Authorization    | Attribute-based (ABAC) within Spring Security DSL |\n   161→| PCI DSS          | Cardholder data tokenized, no PAN stored          |\n   162→| GDPR             | PII encryption at rest (AES-256), Right-to-Erase  |\n   163→| Audit Logging    | Tamper-proof, append-only `audit_log` table       |\n   164→\n   165→---\n   166→\n   167→## 10. Extensibility Guidelines\n   168→\n   169→• **SPI Pattern**: Implement new PSP by dropping a `PaymentProvider` bean on the classpath.  \n   170→• **Domain Events**: Prefer `@DomainEvent` over synchronous calls to reduce coupling.  \n   171→• **Schema Evolutions**: Use Liquibase change-sets; no `ALTER TABLE` directly in prod.  \n   172→• **Customization**: UI allows plug-in Vue components via remote module federation.\n   173→\n   174→---\n   175→\n   176→## 11. Known Trade-offs & Future Work\n   177→\n   178→| Decision                     | Pros                                         | Cons                                         |\n   179→|------------------------------|----------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------|\n   180→| Monolith vs Micro-services   | Simplified ops, ACID, cohesive releases      | Large blast radius, longer startup time      |\n   181→| Redis Streams over Kafka     | Fewer moving parts, lower latency            | No native replay across envs, single writer  |\n   182→| Package-by-component         | High cohesion, discoverability               | Initial learning curve for teams             |\n   183→\n   184→---\n   185→\n   186→## 12. Getting Started for Developers\n   187→\n   188→```bash\n   189→# Clone & build\n   190→git clone git@github.com:commercesphere/enterprise-suite.git\n   191→cd enterprise-suite\n   192→mvn clean package -DskipTests\n   193→\n   194→# Run via Docker Compose\n   195→docker compose up -d db redis\n   196→java -jar ces-app/target/ces-app.jar\n   197→```\n   198→\n   199→Access:\n   200→* Storefront: `http://localhost:8080/`\n   201→* Admin UI: `http://localhost:8080/admin/` (user/pass: `admin/welcome1`)\n   202→\n   203→---\n   204→\n   205→## 13. Appendix\n   206→\n   207→### 13.1 Key Spring Profiles\n   208→\n   209→| Profile      | Purpose                         |\n   210→|--------------|---------------------------------|\n   211→| `local`      | H2 in-memory DB, MailHog        |\n   212→| `staging`    | Full stack, sandbox PSP creds   |\n   213→| `production` | PCI lockdown, readonly Actuator |\n   214→\n   215→### 13.2 Glossary\n   216→\n   217→| Term   | Definition                                          |\n   218→|--------|-----------------------------------------------------|\n   219→| PSP    | Payment Service Provider                            |\n   220→| SAGA   | Long-lived transaction pattern with compensations   |\n   221→| ABAC   | Attribute-Based Access Control                      |\n   222→\n   223→---\n   224→\n   225→© 2024 CommerceSphere Inc. All rights reserved.\n   226→```\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. Executive Summary\nCommerceSphere Enterprise Suite (CES) is an **all-in-one B2B commerce platform** delivered as a single JVM-based deployable. The monolithic approach intentionally contrasts with micro-service sprawl, simplifying governance while retaining **strong modular boundaries** enforced through *package-by-component* architecture, Spring Boot’s hierarchical contexts, and a shared JPA domain model.\n\n---\n\n## 2. High-Level Logical Architecture\n\n```mermaid\ngraph TD\n    subgraph Client Tier\n        A1(Browser) -->|HTTPS| B1[API Gateway<br>Spring MVC]\n        A2(Mobile/Tablet) -->|HTTPS| B1\n    end\n    subgraph Application JVM\n        B1 --> C1[Auth Middleware<br>Spring Security]\n        C1 --> C2[REST Controllers]\n        C2 --> C3[Service Layer<br/>Transactional Façades]\n        C3 --> C4[Domain Model<br/>JPA Entities]\n        C3 --> C5[Payment Orchestrator]\n        C3 --> C6[Pricing & Contracts Engine]\n        C3 --> C7[Approval Workflow Engine]\n    end\n    C4 --> D1[(PostgreSQL)]\n    C5 --> D2[[External PSPs]]\n    C7 --> D3[(Redis Queue)]\n```\n\n* All layers run **in-process** to avoid network overhead.  \n* Public and admin UIs use the same REST resources safeguarded by **role-based ACLs**.  \n* **Spring Transactional** boundaries encapsulate service operations, guaranteeing atomic DB writes and eventual consistency with PSPs via SAGA compensations.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Component Decomposition\n\n| Module                     | Maven GAV (`groupId:artifactId`)  | Responsibility                                                  |\n|----------------------------|-----------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------|\n| `ces-core`                | `io.commercesphere:ces-core`       | Shared domain model, enumerations, and value objects            |\n| `ces-auth`                | `io.commercesphere:ces-auth`       | JWT/OAuth2 flows, SAML integration, MFA                         |\n| `ces-payment`             | `io.commercesphere:ces-payment`    | PSP adapters (Adyen, Stripe, Authorize.Net), PCI token vault    |\n| `ces-pricing`             | `io.commercesphere:ces-pricing`    | Tiered/contract pricing, promotions, taxes                      |\n| `ces-workflow`            | `io.commercesphere:ces-workflow`   | BPMN-based approval and quote workflow engine                   |\n| `ces-admin-ui`            | `io.commercesphere:ces-admin-ui`   | Thymeleaf SPA for ops, finance, and audit                       |\n| `ces-public-ui`           | `io.commercesphere:ces-public-ui`  | Storefront, search, cart, checkout                              |\n\n> NOTE: Even in a monolith, *module jars* enforce clear **compile-time boundaries** to combat “big-ball-of-mud” risks.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Request Lifecycle Walkthrough (`Place Order`)\n\n1. **Browser** posts `/api/v1/orders` with `Authorization: Bearer <JWT>`.\n2. Spring Security’s `JwtAuthenticationFilter` populates `SecurityContext`.\n3. `OrderController#createOrder` receives DTO, delegates to `OrderService`.\n4. `OrderService`:\n   a. Locks inventory via `InventoryService.lock(sku, qty)`.  \n   b. Computes contract price via `PricingFacade#getPrice(...)`.  \n   c. Persists `Order` aggregate (`@Transactional`).  \n   d. Hands off payment to `PaymentFacade` (asynchronous SAGA start).  \n5. `PaymentFacade` pushes message to *Redis Stream* for background capture.  \n6. Controller returns **202 Accepted** with tracking URL `/orders/{id}`.  \n7. Background worker finalizes payment, triggers `OrderPaidEvent`.  \n8. Event listener marks order `PAID`, publishes WebSocket notification.\n\nSequence Diagram *(simplified)*:\n\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n    participant Client\n    participant Controller\n    participant Service\n    participant Payment\n    participant DB\n\n    Client->>Controller: POST /orders\n    Controller->>Service: createOrder(dto)\n    Service->>DB: INSERT order\n    Service->>Payment: initiateCapture(orderId)\n    Payment-->>DB: UPDATE order status\n    Controller-->>Client: 202 Accepted\n```\n\n---\n\n## 5. Data Model Highlights\n\n• **Order Aggregate**  \n```\nOrder 1─* OrderLine\nOrder 1─1 ShippingAddress\nOrder 1─1 Invoice\n```\n\n• **Price Rule Engine** uses *rule chains* persisted as JSON blobs enabling **hot-swapping** without redeploy.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Technology & Frameworks\n\n| Concern            | Technology                         | Justification                                          |\n|--------------------|------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------|\n| DI / Bootstrapping | Spring Boot 3.x                    | Mature ecosystem, Actuator, Observability              |\n| ORM                | Hibernate 6 + Jakarta Persistence  | Rich mapping, batching                                 |\n| DB                 | PostgreSQL 15                      | ACID, native JSONB                                     |\n| Caching            | Redis 7 (Cluster)                  | Low-latency catalog & session caching                  |\n| Messaging          | Redis Streams                      | Lightweight, in-JVM simplicity                         |\n| Build              | Maven Centralized BOM             | Reproducible builds, SBOM generation                   |\n| CI/CD              | GitHub Actions → Artifactory → K8s | Policy-driven pipelines, image scanning (Trivy)        |\n| Observability      | OpenTelemetry, Grafana, ELK        | Distributed tracing, log correlation                   |\n\n---\n\n## 7. Deployment Topology\n\n```mermaid\nflowchart LR\n    subgraph k8s-cluster\n        app[ces-app:war<br/>Tomcat 10]:::pod\n        redis[(Redis Cluster)]:::svc\n        db[(Postgres HA)]:::svc\n        ingress[[NGINX Ingress]]:::svc\n    end\n    classDef pod fill:#c6e5ff,stroke:#036,border-width:1px;\n    classDef svc fill:#fde3a7,stroke:#b36,stroke-width:1px;\n\n    ingress --> app\n    app --> redis\n    app --> db\n```\n\n* Stateless app pods enable **horizontal scaling**.  \n* Persistent data lives solely in PostgreSQL; Redis is a cache/queue and can be rebuilt.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Error Handling & Resilience\n\n1. **Controller Advice** (`@RestControllerAdvice`) translates exceptions to RFC-7807 *Problem Details* JSON.  \n2. **Retry Template** wraps outbound PSP calls (`maxAttempts=3`, exp back-off).  \n3. Non-recoverable errors push to **Dead Letter Stream** for ops triage.  \n4. Circuit Breakers (`Resilience4j`) guard fragile integrations.\n\n---\n\n## 9. Security & Compliance\n\n| Area             | Initiative                                        |\n|------------------|---------------------------------------------------|\n| Authentication   | OAuth2 + SAML SLO, enforced MFA (TOTP)            |\n| Authorization    | Attribute-based (ABAC) within Spring Security DSL |\n| PCI DSS          | Cardholder data tokenized, no PAN stored          |\n| GDPR             | PII encryption at rest (AES-256), Right-to-Erase  |\n| Audit Logging    | Tamper-proof, append-only `audit_log` table       |\n\n---\n\n## 10. Extensibility Guidelines\n\n• **SPI Pattern**: Implement new PSP by dropping a `PaymentProvider` bean on the classpath.  \n• **Domain Events**: Prefer `@DomainEvent` over synchronous calls to reduce coupling.  \n• **Schema Evolutions**: Use Liquibase change-sets; no `ALTER TABLE` directly in prod.  \n• **Customization**: UI allows plug-in Vue components via remote module federation.\n\n---\n\n## 11. Known Trade-offs & Future Work\n\n| Decision                     | Pros                                         | Cons                                         |\n|------------------------------|----------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------|\n| Monolith vs Micro-services   | Simplified ops, ACID, cohesive releases      | Large blast radius, longer startup time      |\n| Redis Streams over Kafka     | Fewer moving parts, lower latency            | No native replay across envs, single writer  |\n| Package-by-component         | High cohesion, discoverability               | Initial learning curve for teams             |\n\n---\n\n## 12. Getting Started for Developers\n\n```bash\n# Clone & build\ngit clone git@github.com:commercesphere/enterprise-suite.git\ncd enterprise-suite\nmvn clean package -DskipTests\n\n# Run via Docker Compose\ndocker compose up -d db redis\njava -jar ces-app/target/ces-app.jar\n```\n\nAccess:\n* Storefront: `http://localhost:8080/`\n* Admin UI: `http://localhost:8080/admin/` (user/pass: `admin/welcome1`)\n\n---\n\n## 13. Appendix\n\n### 13.1 Key Spring Profiles\n\n| Profile      | Purpose                         |\n|--------------|---------------------------------|\n| `local`      | H2 in-memory DB, MailHog        |\n| `staging`    | Full stack, sandbox PSP creds   |\n| `production` | PCI lockdown, readonly Actuator |\n\n### 13.2 Glossary\n\n| Term   | Definition                                          |\n|--------|-----------------------------------------------------|\n| PSP    | Payment Service Provider                            |\n| SAGA   | Long-lived transaction pattern with compensations   |\n| ABAC   | Attribute-Based Access Control                      |\n\n---\n\n© 2024 CommerceSphere Inc. All rights reserved.\n```","numLines":226,"startLine":1,"totalLines":226}}}

Context  \n\nCommerceSphere Enterprise Suite (CSES) targets mid-to-large B2B wholesalers that require:\n\n* Contract-driven, tiered pricing with sub-millisecond look-ups  \n* Complex quote-to-cash workflows (RFQ ➟ Quote ➟ PO ➟ Invoice)  \n* Centralised compliance & audit logging (PCI-DSS, SOX, GDPR)  \n* Coordinated feature rollouts across Storefront, Admin, and Public APIs  \n* Lean IT departments with limited DevOps maturity\n\nHistorically, the team experimented with a *micro-service* PoC. That prototype surfaced the following pain points:\n\n| Pain Point                              | Impact                                                                                       |\n|-----------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\n| Siloed data models                      | Duplicate catalog & pricing schemas ➟ consistency bugs                                       |\n| Cross-service transactions              | 2PC & Sagas introduced operational complexity                                                |\n| Release coordination overhead           | “Version drift” across 14 repos blocked RFC-312 features                                     |\n| Compliance & audit trail fragmentation  | Log correlation required Elastic/Jaeger, out of scope for target customers’ budgets         |\n| Latency budget violations               | Inventory lock/unlock flows breached 50 ms budget due to network hops                       |\n\nGiven the customer base, delivery cadence, and in-house skill set, **a single deployable artifact with modular boundaries** is preferable.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Decision  \n\nWe will implement CommerceSphere as a **Modular Monolith** running inside one JVM and backed by one relational schema.\n\nHigh-level stack:\n\n```\n┌──────────────────────────┐\n│      Presentation        │  MVC (Thymeleaf)  |  REST (Spring Web)  |  GraphQL (Apollo)\n├──────────────────────────┤\n│     Application Layer    │  Spring Services, CQRS Commands/Queries\n├──────────────────────────┤\n│        Domain            │  DDD Aggregates, Validation, Pricing Engine\n├──────────────────────────┤\n│     Infrastructure       │  Spring Data JPA, Kafka Adapter, Payment PSP SDKs\n└──────────────────────────┘\n```\n\nModularity is enforced by:\n\n1. Maven multi-module build (`cses-domain`, `cses-admin-ui`, `cses-payment`, …)  \n2. IntelliJ/Gradle *enforced package boundaries* (`com.commercesphere.<module>.**`)  \n3. Hexagonal Architecture ports/adapters for external systems (Payments, ERP, WMS)\n\n---\n\n## 3. Drivers  \n\n1. **Feature Cohesion** – Contract, pricing, and inventory must update atomically.  \n2. **Operational Simplicity** – Single artifact to deploy on customer-managed K8s or VM.  \n3. **Performance** – In-process calls (< 1 µs) vs gRPC/REST (≥ 1 ms).  \n4. **Audit & Compliance** – One log stream simplifies evidence collection.  \n5. **Team Size** – 8 engineers; Conway’s law suggests one product ≈ one repo.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Considered Alternatives  \n\n| Alternative       | Pros                                         | Cons                                                       | Verdict |\n|-------------------|----------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------|---------|\n| Pure Micro-services | Independent scaling; tech polyglot           | High ops cost; distributed transactions; slower time-to-market | ❌ |\n| Self-Contained Systems (SCS) | Clear domain boundaries; limited coupling | Duplication of shared libs; still multiple deployables        | ❌ |\n| Modular Monolith  | Cohesive deployment; in-process speed        | Requires discipline to avoid crossing module boundaries    | ✅ |\n\n---\n\n## 5. Consequences  \n\n### Positive  \n\n* **Deployment** – Helm chart with single `Deployment` and `Service`.  \n* **Observability** – One Datadog JVM APM agent, one Grafana dashboard.  \n* **Release Cadence** – `git tag vYYYY.MM.DD`. Blue/Green deploy via Rollout.  \n\n### Negative / Mitigations  \n\n| Risk                          | Mitigation                                           |\n|-------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------|\n| Module bleeding (tight coupling) | Checkstyle + ArchUnit in CI; “Public API” packages only |\n| JVM horizontal scaling limits | Stateless service layer; Enable clustering (Hazelcast) |\n| Large build times             | Gradle’s configuration caching; module-level parallelism |\n\n---\n\n## 6. Compliance Footprint  \n\n* PCI-DSS SAQ-D: encrypted card vault with tokenisation (`cses-payment`)  \n* GDPR Article 30: Data-lineage logging module (`cses-audit`)  \n* SOX §404: Immutable audit events persisted via `Postgres->Debezium->S3`\n\n---\n\n## 7. Impacted Codebase Policies  \n\n1. Each Maven module exports a `*-api` JAR; dependencies across modules **must** use only exported interfaces.  \n2. Spring Boot’s component scan is **limited per module** via `@ComponentScan(\"com.commercesphere.<module>..\")`.  \n3. Feature toggles handled centrally via FF4J; toggles **must not** reside in module-local configs to prevent orphaned flags.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Test Strategy  \n\n* **Unit Tests** – JUnit 5 + Mockito for isolated domain logic.  \n* **Contract Tests** – Spring Cloud Contract ensures REST compatibility for external integrators.  \n* **Component Tests** – Testcontainers spins up Postgres, Kafka, and Stripe mock inside one JVM.  \n* **End-to-End** – Cypress triggers UI flows against single deployed artifact.\n\n---\n\n## 9. Open Issues  \n\n1. Evaluate Java Platform Module System (JPMS) enforcement in JDK 22.  \n2. Investigate GraalVM native image for lambda-style edge deployments.\n\n---\n\n## 10. References  \n\n* Nygard, M. “Monolith First” – https://michaelnygard.com/blog/2015/04/monolith-first/  \n* ThoughtWorks Tech Radar 2023 Q4 – *“Modular Monoliths hold micro-services at bay.”*  \n\n---\n\n```java\n// Sample ArchUnit rule (cses-architecture-tests)\n@AnalyzeClasses(packages = \"com.commercesphere\")\nclass ModuleBoundariesTest {\n\n    @ArchTest\n    static final ArchRule modules_should_not_depend_on_each_other =\n            slices().matching(\"com.commercesphere.(*)..\")\n                    .should().beFreeOfCycles()\n                    .because(\"cyclic dependencies indicate leaked abstractions\");\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n*Last reviewed by: Platform Architecture Guild – 2024-05-20*  \n```","numLines":150,"startLine":1,"totalLines":150}}}

Status\n    13→ACCEPTED – This decision has been implemented starting with version **7.4.0** of CommerceSphere Enterprise Suite.\n    14→\n    15→## 2. Context\n    16→\n    17→Historically, CommerceSphere has been organized using the traditional **package-by-layer** convention:\n    18→\n    19→```\n    20→com.commercesphere\n    21→  └── catalog\n    22→      ├── controller\n    23→      ├── service\n    24→      ├── repository\n    25→      └── model\n    26→```\n    27→\n    28→While this structure is intuitive for newcomers, we have observed several pain points over the last three release cycles:\n    29→\n    30→* **Poor Feature Cohesion** – The code for a single functional slice (e.g., *Quote Management*) is scattered across many packages.\n    31→* **High Cognitive Load** – Engineers must open multiple folders to debug or extend a single feature.\n    32→* **Bloated Merge Conflicts** – Trivial changes in one layer often collide with unrelated commits in the same layer.\n    33→* **Opaque Ownership** – It is hard to determine which squad owns which classes when the same package layer hosts multiple features.\n    34→\n    35→Given the accelerating pace of delivery (now shipping **bi-weekly**), we require a more cohesive, maintainable structure.\n    36→\n    37→## 3. Decision\n    38→\n    39→We will adopt a **Package-by-Feature** organization as the default for all new code and progressively migrate existing packages.\n    40→\n    41→```\n    42→com.commercesphere\n    43→  └── features\n    44→      ├── catalog            # Product catalogs & facets\n    45→      │   ├── CatalogController.java\n    46→      │   ├── CatalogService.java\n    47→      │   ├── CatalogRepository.java\n    48→      │   └── CatalogMapper.java\n    49→      │\n    50→      ├── payments           # Payment orchestration\n    51→      │   ├── PaymentsController.java\n    52→      │   ├── PaymentsService.java\n    53→      │   ├── PaymentsFacade.java\n    54→      │   └── PaymentsRepository.java\n    55→      │\n    56→      └── quote              # Quote-to-cash workflows\n    57→          ├── QuoteController.java\n    58→          ├── QuoteService.java\n    59→          ├── QuoteRepository.java\n    60→          └── QuotePolicyValidator.java\n    61→```\n    62→\n    63→### 3.1 Enforcement\n    64→\n    65→1. Code reviews will reject new classes that are added directly under the top-level `controller`, `service`, or `repository` packages.\n    66→2. A Gradle task (`enforcePackageByFeature`) has been introduced to fail the build when violations are detected by static analysis (`ArchUnit` rules).\n    67→3. Each feature must include a private, `package-private` factory to prevent cross-feature leakage of internal abstractions.\n    68→\n    69→## 4. Consequences\n    70→\n    71→### Positive\n    72→\n    73→* **High Cohesion & Low Coupling** – All classes that contribute to a feature reside in one folder.\n    74→* **Streamlined Git Diffs** – Most changes touch only a small surface area.\n    75→* **Feature-level CI Validation** – Tests, QA data, and mocks are co-located with code, enabling targeted test runs (`gradle :features:payments:test`).\n    76→* **Clear Ownership** – Squads can “own” a package without affecting others.\n    77→\n    78→### Negative\n    79→\n    80→* **Potential Classpath Confusion** – Import statements become longer (`com.commercesphere.features.catalog.*`).\n    81→* **Migration Overhead** – Moving existing classes may break binary compatibility for integrators using reflection.\n    82→* **IDE Index Impact** – Large IDEs (e.g., IntelliJ) may take longer to re-index if package names are heavily mutated.\n    83→\n    84→## 5. Alternatives Considered\n    85→\n    86→| Alternative | Outcome |\n    87→|-------------|---------|\n    88→| Continue with Package-by-Layer | Rejected – Does not address identified pain points. |\n    89→| Split into Micro-Services | Rejected – Violates monolithic cohesion requirement & increases operational overhead. |\n    90→| Adopt Java 17 Modules | Rejected for now – Adds complexity and would require major refactoring in build pipelines. |\n    91→\n    92→## 6. Example Implementation\n    93→\n    94→Below is a minimal yet functional **Quote** feature demonstrating the new structure.\n    95→\n    96→### 6.1 `QuoteController.java`\n    97→```java\n    98→package com.commercesphere.features.quote;\n    99→\n   100→import jakarta.validation.Valid;\n   101→import org.springframework.http.HttpStatus;\n   102→import org.springframework.http.ResponseEntity;\n   103→import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.*;\n   104→import java.util.UUID;\n   105→\n   106→/**\n   107→ * Public REST endpoint for Quote operations.\n   108→ */\n   109→@RestController\n   110→@RequestMapping(\"/api/v1/quotes\")\n   111→class QuoteController {\n   112→\n   113→    private final QuoteService quoteService;\n   114→    private final QuoteAssembler assembler;\n   115→\n   116→    QuoteController(QuoteService quoteService, QuoteAssembler assembler) {\n   117→        this.quoteService = quoteService;\n   118→        this.assembler = assembler;\n   119→    }\n   120→\n   121→    @PostMapping\n   122→    ResponseEntity<QuoteView> createQuote(@Valid @RequestBody QuoteRequest request) {\n   123→        Quote quote = quoteService.createQuote(request);\n   124→        return ResponseEntity\n   125→                .status(HttpStatus.CREATED)\n   126→                .body(assembler.toView(quote));\n   127→    }\n   128→\n   129→    @GetMapping(\"/{id}\")\n   130→    ResponseEntity<QuoteView> getQuote(@PathVariable UUID id) {\n   131→        return quoteService.getQuote(id)\n   132→                .map(assembler::toView)\n   133→                .map(ResponseEntity::ok)\n   134→                .orElse(ResponseEntity.notFound().build());\n   135→    }\n   136→}\n   137→```\n   138→\n   139→### 6.2 `QuoteService.java`\n   140→```java\n   141→package com.commercesphere.features.quote;\n   142→\n   143→import java.util.Optional;\n   144→import java.util.UUID;\n   145→\n   146→/**\n   147→ * Domain service containing Quote business logic.\n   148→ */\n   149→interface QuoteService {\n   150→\n   151→    Quote createQuote(QuoteRequest request);\n   152→\n   153→    Optional<Quote> getQuote(UUID id);\n   154→}\n   155→```\n   156→\n   157→### 6.3 `DefaultQuoteService.java`\n   158→```java\n   159→package com.commercesphere.features.quote;\n   160→\n   161→import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;\n   162→import org.springframework.transaction.annotation.Transactional;\n   163→import java.util.Optional;\n   164→import java.util.UUID;\n   165→\n   166→@Service\n   167→class DefaultQuoteService implements QuoteService {\n   168→\n   169→    private final QuoteRepository repository;\n   170→    private final QuotePolicyValidator policyValidator;\n   171→\n   172→    DefaultQuoteService(QuoteRepository repository,\n   173→                        QuotePolicyValidator policyValidator) {\n   174→        this.repository = repository;\n   175→        this.policyValidator = policyValidator;\n   176→    }\n   177→\n   178→    @Override\n   179→    @Transactional\n   180→    public Quote createQuote(QuoteRequest request) {\n   181→        policyValidator.validate(request);\n   182→        Quote quote = Quote.of(request);\n   183→        return repository.save(quote);\n   184→    }\n   185→\n   186→    @Override\n   187→    @Transactional(readOnly = true)\n   188→    public Optional<Quote> getQuote(UUID id) {\n   189→        return repository.findById(id);\n   190→    }\n   191→}\n   192→```\n   193→\n   194→### 6.4 `QuoteRepository.java`\n   195→```java\n   196→package com.commercesphere.features.quote;\n   197→\n   198→import org.springframework.data.jpa.repository.JpaRepository;\n   199→import java.util.UUID;\n   200→\n   201→/**\n   202→ * Spring Data repository for Quote entities.\n   203→ */\n   204→interface QuoteRepository extends JpaRepository<Quote, UUID> {\n   205→}\n   206→```\n   207→\n   208→### 6.5 `QuotePolicyValidator.java`\n   209→```java\n   210→package com.commercesphere.features.quote;\n   211→\n   212→import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;\n   213→\n   214→/**\n   215→ * Validates quote creation policies such as tiered pricing\n   216→ * and customer approval workflow.\n   217→ */\n   218→@Component\n   219→class QuotePolicyValidator {\n   220→\n   221→    void validate(QuoteRequest request) {\n   222→        // Business rule: Minimum order amount must exceed contract threshold\n   223→        if (request.totalValue().compareTo(request.contractThreshold()) < 0) {\n   224→            throw new IllegalStateException(\n   225→                \"Quote does not satisfy minimum contract threshold\");\n   226→        }\n   227→    }\n   228→}\n   229→```\n   230→\n   231→### 6.6 `QuoteAssembler.java`\n   232→```java\n   233→package com.commercesphere.features.quote;\n   234→\n   235→import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;\n   236→\n   237→/**\n   238→ * Converts between domain objects and DTOs.\n   239→ */\n   240→@Component\n   241→class QuoteAssembler {\n   242→\n   243→    QuoteView toView(Quote quote) {\n   244→        return new QuoteView(\n   245→                quote.id(),\n   246→                quote.status(),\n   247→                quote.totalValue()\n   248→        );\n   249→    }\n   250→}\n   251→```\n   252→\n   253→> NOTE: Unit tests for each class reside in the same package (`src/test/java/com/commercesphere/features/quote`) to leverage package-private methods.\n   254→\n   255→## 7. Migration Plan\n   256→\n   257→1. **Sprint 62** – Migrate features `catalog`, `payments`, and `quote`.\n   258→2. **Sprint 63** – Auto-refactor layer packages (`controller`, `service`, etc.) using IntelliJ structural search.\n   259→3. **Sprint 64** – Remove deprecated packages and enable `enforcePackageByFeature` build rule in **fail-fast** mode.\n   260→4. Document the new structure in the developer playbook & host a lunch-and-learn.\n   261→\n   262→## 8. References\n   263→\n   264→* “Package by Feature, not Layer” by Robert C. Status\nACCEPTED – This decision has been implemented starting with version **7.4.0** of CommerceSphere Enterprise Suite.\n\n## 2. Context\n\nHistorically, CommerceSphere has been organized using the traditional **package-by-layer** convention:\n\n```\ncom.commercesphere\n  └── catalog\n      ├── controller\n      ├── service\n      ├── repository\n      └── model\n```\n\nWhile this structure is intuitive for newcomers, we have observed several pain points over the last three release cycles:\n\n* **Poor Feature Cohesion** – The code for a single functional slice (e.g., *Quote Management*) is scattered across many packages.\n* **High Cognitive Load** – Engineers must open multiple folders to debug or extend a single feature.\n* **Bloated Merge Conflicts** – Trivial changes in one layer often collide with unrelated commits in the same layer.\n* **Opaque Ownership** – It is hard to determine which squad owns which classes when the same package layer hosts multiple features.\n\nGiven the accelerating pace of delivery (now shipping **bi-weekly**), we require a more cohesive, maintainable structure.\n\n## 3. Decision\n\nWe will adopt a **Package-by-Feature** organization as the default for all new code and progressively migrate existing packages.\n\n```\ncom.commercesphere\n  └── features\n      ├── catalog            # Product catalogs & facets\n      │   ├── CatalogController.java\n      │   ├── CatalogService.java\n      │   ├── CatalogRepository.java\n      │   └── CatalogMapper.java\n      │\n      ├── payments           # Payment orchestration\n      │   ├── PaymentsController.java\n      │   ├── PaymentsService.java\n      │   ├── PaymentsFacade.java\n      │   └── PaymentsRepository.java\n      │\n      └── quote              # Quote-to-cash workflows\n          ├── QuoteController.java\n          ├── QuoteService.java\n          ├── QuoteRepository.java\n          └── QuotePolicyValidator.java\n```\n\n### 3.1 Enforcement\n\n1. Code reviews will reject new classes that are added directly under the top-level `controller`, `service`, or `repository` packages.\n2. A Gradle task (`enforcePackageByFeature`) has been introduced to fail the build when violations are detected by static analysis (`ArchUnit` rules).\n3. Each feature must include a private, `package-private` factory to prevent cross-feature leakage of internal abstractions.\n\n## 4. Consequences\n\n### Positive\n\n* **High Cohesion & Low Coupling** – All classes that contribute to a feature reside in one folder.\n* **Streamlined Git Diffs** – Most changes touch only a small surface area.\n* **Feature-level CI Validation** – Tests, QA data, and mocks are co-located with code, enabling targeted test runs (`gradle :features:payments:test`).\n* **Clear Ownership** – Squads can “own” a package without affecting others.\n\n### Negative\n\n* **Potential Classpath Confusion** – Import statements become longer (`com.commercesphere.features.catalog.*`).\n* **Migration Overhead** – Moving existing classes may break binary compatibility for integrators using reflection.\n* **IDE Index Impact** – Large IDEs (e.g., IntelliJ) may take longer to re-index if package names are heavily mutated.\n\n## 5. Alternatives Considered\n\n| Alternative | Outcome |\n|-------------|---------|\n| Continue with Package-by-Layer | Rejected – Does not address identified pain points. |\n| Split into Micro-Services | Rejected – Violates monolithic cohesion requirement & increases operational overhead. |\n| Adopt Java 17 Modules | Rejected for now – Adds complexity and would require major refactoring in build pipelines. |\n\n## 6. Example Implementation\n\nBelow is a minimal yet functional **Quote** feature demonstrating the new structure.\n\n### 6.1 `QuoteController.java`\n```java\npackage com.commercesphere.features.quote;\n\nimport jakarta.validation.Valid;\nimport org.springframework.http.HttpStatus;\nimport org.springframework.http.ResponseEntity;\nimport org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.*;\nimport java.util.UUID;\n\n/**\n * Public REST endpoint for Quote operations.\n */\n@RestController\n@RequestMapping(\"/api/v1/quotes\")\nclass QuoteController {\n\n    private final QuoteService quoteService;\n    private final QuoteAssembler assembler;\n\n    QuoteController(QuoteService quoteService, QuoteAssembler assembler) {\n        this.quoteService = quoteService;\n        this.assembler = assembler;\n    }\n\n    @PostMapping\n    ResponseEntity<QuoteView> createQuote(@Valid @RequestBody QuoteRequest request) {\n        Quote quote = quoteService.createQuote(request);\n        return ResponseEntity\n                .status(HttpStatus.CREATED)\n                .body(assembler.toView(quote));\n    }\n\n    @GetMapping(\"/{id}\")\n    ResponseEntity<QuoteView> getQuote(@PathVariable UUID id) {\n        return quoteService.getQuote(id)\n                .map(assembler::toView)\n                .map(ResponseEntity::ok)\n                .orElse(ResponseEntity.notFound().build());\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 6.2 `QuoteService.java`\n```java\npackage com.commercesphere.features.quote;\n\nimport java.util.Optional;\nimport java.util.UUID;\n\n/**\n * Domain service containing Quote business logic.\n */\ninterface QuoteService {\n\n    Quote createQuote(QuoteRequest request);\n\n    Optional<Quote> getQuote(UUID id);\n}\n```\n\n### 6.3 `DefaultQuoteService.java`\n```java\npackage com.commercesphere.features.quote;\n\nimport org.springframework.stereotype.Service;\nimport org.springframework.transaction.annotation.Transactional;\nimport java.util.Optional;\nimport java.util.UUID;\n\n@Service\nclass DefaultQuoteService implements QuoteService {\n\n    private final QuoteRepository repository;\n    private final QuotePolicyValidator policyValidator;\n\n    DefaultQuoteService(QuoteRepository repository,\n                        QuotePolicyValidator policyValidator) {\n        this.repository = repository;\n        this.policyValidator = policyValidator;\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    @Transactional\n    public Quote createQuote(QuoteRequest request) {\n        policyValidator.validate(request);\n        Quote quote = Quote.of(request);\n        return repository.save(quote);\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    @Transactional(readOnly = true)\n    public Optional<Quote> getQuote(UUID id) {\n        return repository.findById(id);\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 6.4 `QuoteRepository.java`\n```java\npackage com.commercesphere.features.quote;\n\nimport org.springframework.data.jpa.repository.JpaRepository;\nimport java.util.UUID;\n\n/**\n * Spring Data repository for Quote entities.\n */\ninterface QuoteRepository extends JpaRepository<Quote, UUID> {\n}\n```\n\n### 6.5 `QuotePolicyValidator.java`\n```java\npackage com.commercesphere.features.quote;\n\nimport org.springframework.stereotype.Component;\n\n/**\n * Validates quote creation policies such as tiered pricing\n * and customer approval workflow.\n */\n@Component\nclass QuotePolicyValidator {\n\n    void validate(QuoteRequest request) {\n        // Business rule: Minimum order amount must exceed contract threshold\n        if (request.totalValue().compareTo(request.contractThreshold()) < 0) {\n            throw new IllegalStateException(\n                \"Quote does not satisfy minimum contract threshold\");\n        }\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 6.6 `QuoteAssembler.java`\n```java\npackage com.commercesphere.features.quote;\n\nimport org.springframework.stereotype.Component;\n\n/**\n * Converts between domain objects and DTOs.\n */\n@Component\nclass QuoteAssembler {\n\n    QuoteView toView(Quote quote) {\n        return new QuoteView(\n                quote.id(),\n                quote.status(),\n                quote.totalValue()\n        );\n    }\n}\n```\n\n> NOTE: Unit tests for each class reside in the same package (`src/test/java/com/commercesphere/features/quote`) to leverage package-private methods.\n\n## 7. Migration Plan\n\n1. **Sprint 62** – Migrate features `catalog`, `payments`, and `quote`.\n2. **Sprint 63** – Auto-refactor layer packages (`controller`, `service`, etc.) using IntelliJ structural search.\n3. **Sprint 64** – Remove deprecated packages and enable `enforcePackageByFeature` build rule in **fail-fast** mode.\n4. Document the new structure in the developer playbook & host a lunch-and-learn.\n\n## 8. References\n\n* “Package by Feature, not Layer” by Robert C. Martin – NDC Conference 2017.\n* ArchUnit Rule Examples – https://www.archunit.org\n* Shopify Engineering – *Organizing Rails Controllers by Feature* (analogous concept)\n\n---\nThis ADR will be revisited after two major releases (approx. 6 months) to assess its efficacy.\n```","numLines":270,"startLine":1,"totalLines":270}}}

the persistence layer (repositories) from callers and\n * ensures that all domain invariants are upheld before state transitions are\n * committed. Declarative transaction boundaries guarantee atomicity and\n * consistency across multi-table writes (order header, line items, payment\n * ledger).</p>\n */\n@Service\n@Validated\npublic class OrderService {\n\n    private static final Logger log = LoggerFactory.getLogger(OrderService.class);\n\n    private final OrderRepository orderRepository;\n    private final ProductService productService;\n    private final PaymentService paymentService;\n\n    @Autowired\n    public OrderService(OrderRepository orderRepository,\n                        ProductService productService,\n                        PaymentService paymentService) {\n        this.orderRepository = Objects.requireNonNull(orderRepository, \"orderRepository\");\n        this.productService = Objects.requireNonNull(productService, \"productService\");\n        this.paymentService = Objects.requireNonNull(paymentService, \"paymentService\");\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Begins a new draft order for the supplied customer.\n     */\n    @Transactional\n    public Order createOrder(@NotNull Long customerId) {\n        Order order = new Order();\n        order.setCustomerId(customerId);\n        order.setStatus(OrderStatus.DRAFT);\n        order.setCreatedAt(OffsetDateTime.now());\n        order.setLastModifiedAt(order.getCreatedAt());\n        Order persisted = orderRepository.save(order);\n        log.info(\"Created draft order [{}] for customer [{}]\", persisted.getId(), customerId);\n        return persisted;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Adds a product to an existing draft order. If the product already exists\n     * in the order, its quantity is incremented rather than creating a new line\n     * item.\n     *\n     * @throws ResourceNotFoundException  if order or product are missing\n     * @throws BusinessValidationException if order is not in a mutable state\n     */\n    @Transactional\n    public Order addItem(@NotNull Long orderId,\n                         @NotNull Long productId,\n                         @Min(1) int quantity) {\n        Order order = fetchMutableOrder(orderId);\n        Product product = productService.findProductById(productId)\n li.getProductId().equals(productId))\n                .findFirst()\n                .orElseGet(() -> {\n                    OrderItem newItem = new OrderItem();\n                    newItem.setProductId(productId);\n                    newItem.setSku(product.getSku());\n                    newItem.setUnitPrice(product.getPrice());\n                    newItem.setCurrency(product.getCurrency());\n                    order.getItems().add(newItem);\n                    return newItem;\n                });\n\n        line.setQuantity(line.getQuantity() + quantity);\n        recalculateTotals(order);\n        order.setLastModifiedAt(OffsetDateTime.now());\n\n        log.debug(\"Added [{}] units of product [{}] to order [{}]\", quantity, productId, orderId);\n        return orderRepository.save(order);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Submits the order for payment authorization and marks it as\n     * PROCESSING if successful.\n     *\n     * @throws BusinessValidationException if payment authorization fails\n     * @throws ResourceNotFoundException   if order not found\n     */\n    @Transactional\n    public Order checkout(@NotNull Long orderId, @NotNull PaymentDetails paymentDetails) {\n        Order order = fetchMutableOrder(orderId);\n\n        if (order.getItems().isEmpty()) {\n            throw new BusinessValidationException(\"Order contains no line items\");\n        }\n\n        recalculateTotals(order);\n\n        PaymentResult paymentResult = paymentService.authorize(paymentDetails, order.getGrandTotal(), order.getCurrency());\n\n        if (!paymentResult.isApproved()) {\n            throw new BusinessValidationException(\"Payment was declined: \" + paymentResult.getDeclineReason());\n        }\n\n        order.setPaymentReference(paymentResult.getPaymentReference());\n        order.setStatus(OrderStatus.PROCESSING);\n        order.setSubmittedAt(OffsetDateTime.now());\n        order.setLastModifiedAt(order.getSubmittedAt());\n\n        log.info(\"Order [{}] successfully checked out. Payment reference [{}]\",\n                orderId, paymentResult.getPaymentReference());\n\n        return orderRepository.save(order);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Cancels an order that has not yet shipped or been fulfilled. All\n     * financial authorizations are voided and inventory is released.\n     */\n    @Transactional\n    public void cancelOrder(@NotNull Long orderId, @NotNull Long cancelledBy) {\n        Order order = orderRepository.findById(orderId)\n                .orElseThrow(() -> new ResourceNotFoundException(\"Order \" + orderId + \" not found\"));\n\n        if (!order.getStatus().isCancellable()) {\n            throw new BusinessValidationException(\n                    \"Order \" + orderId + \" cannot be cancelled in state \" + order.getStatus());\n        }\n\n        order.setStatus(OrderStatus.CANCELLED);\n        order.setCancelledAt(OffsetDateTime.now());\n        order.setCancelledBy(cancelledBy);\n        order.setLastModifiedAt(order.getCancelledAt());\n        orderRepository.save(order);\n\n        // Best-effort void; network issues will raise infra-level exception and trigger retry policy.\n        paymentService.voidAuthorization(order.getPaymentReference());\n\n        log.info(\"Order [{}] has been cancelled by user [{}]\", orderId, cancelledBy);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Retrieves an order by id. Read-only; no transactional boundary needed.\n     */\n    public Order getOrderById(@NotNull Long orderId) {\n        return orderRepository.findById(orderId)\n                .orElseThrow(() -> new ResourceNotFoundException(\"Order \" + orderId + \" not found\"));\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Paginated listing of a customer's orders.\n     */\n    public Page<Order> listOrdersForCustomer(@NotNull Long customerId, Pageable pageable) {\n        return orderRepository.findByCustomerId(customerId, pageable);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Helper that ensures the order exists and is in DRAFT state so it can be\n     * mutated. Propagates a human-readable validation error otherwise.\n     */\n    private Order fetchMutableOrder(Long orderId) {\n        Order order = orderRepository.findById(orderId)\n                .orElseThrow(() -> new ResourceNotFoundException(\"Order \" + orderId + \" not found\"));\n\n        if (order.getStatus() != OrderStatus.DRAFT) {\n            throw new BusinessValidationException(\n                    \"Order \" + orderId + \" is not editable in state \" + order.getStatus());\n        }\n        return order;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Re-calculates order subtotal, tax, and grand total.\n     *\n     * <p>Tax calculation is deliberately simplified here—real implementation\n     * would delegate to a dedicated TaxService that considers jurisdiction,\n     * exemptions, and contract-level overrides.</p>\n     */\n    private void recalculateTotals(Order order) {\n        BigDecimal subtotal = order.getItems().stream()\n                .map(li -> li.getUnitPrice().multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(li.getQuantity())))\n                .reduce(BigDecimal.ZERO, BigDecimal::add);\n\n        BigDecimal estimatedTax = subtotal.multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(0.07)); // 7% flat\n        BigDecimal grandTotal = subtotal.add(estimatedTax);\n\n        order.setSubtotal(subtotal);\n        order.setTaxTotal(estimatedTax);\n        order.setGrandTotal(grandTotal);\n    }\n}","numLines":219,"startLine":1,"totalLines":219}}}

PaymentIntentCreateParams params = buildAuthorizationParams(request);\n            PaymentIntent intent = PaymentIntent.create(params);\n            auditSuccess(\"AUTHORIZE\", intent.getId(), request.getOrderId());\n            return buildPaymentResponse(intent, PaymentStatus.AUTHORIZED);\n        } catch (StripeException e) {\n            auditFailure(\"AUTHORIZE\", request.getOrderId(), Instant.now().toString())\n                    .build();\n\n            intent = intent.capture(captureParams);\n            auditSuccess(\"CAPTURE\", intent.getId(), authorizationId);\n            return buildPaymentResponse(intent, PaymentStatus.CAPTURED);\n        } catch (StripeException e) {\n            auditFailure(\"CAPTURE\", authorizationId, e);\n            throw translateStripeException(\"capture\", e);\n        }\n    }\n\n    private PaymentResponse doRefund(String transactionId,\n                                     BigDecimal amount,\n      .setPaymentIntent(transactionId)\n                    .setReason(RefundCreateParams.Reason.REQUESTED_BY_CUSTOMER)\n                    .putMetadata(\"refunded_at\", Instant.now().toString())\n .setCurrency(request.getCurrency().getCurrencyCode().toLowerCase())\n                .setAmount(toMinorUnit(request.getAmount(), request.getCurrency()))\n                .setCustomer(request.getCustomerReference())\n                .setPaymentMethod(request.getPaymentMethodToken())\n                .setConfirm(Boolean.TRUE)\n                .setCaptureMethod(PaymentIntentCreateParams.CaptureMethod.MANUAL)\n                .setIdempotencyKey(buildIdempotencyKey(\"auth\", request.getOrderId()))\n                .putMetadata(\"order_id\", request.getOrderId())\n                .putMetadata(\"created_at\", Instant.now().toString())\n                .build();\n    }\n\n    private String buildIdempotencyKey(String prefix, String orderId) {\n        // Stripe idempotency keys must be unique for each logical operation.\n        return String.format(\"%s_%s_%s\",\n                prefix,\n                orderId,\n                UUID.randomUUID().toString().replace(\"-\", \"\").substring(0, 18));\n    }\n\n    private long toMinorUnit(BigDecimal amount, Currency currency) {\n        int fraction = currency.getDefaultFractionDigits();\n        return amount.movePointRight(fraction).longValueExact();\n    }\n\n    private PaymentResponse buildPaymentResponse(PaymentIntent intent, PaymentStatus status) {\n        return PaymentResponse.builder()\n                .gatewayTransactionId(intent.getId())\n                .status(status)\n                .amount(new BigDecimal(intent.getAmount()).movePointLeft(intent.getCurrency().equals(\"jpy\") ? 0 : 2))\n                .currency(Currency.getInstance(intent.getCurrency().toUpperCase()))\n                .createdAt(Instant.ofEpochSecond(intent.getCreated()))\n                .rawResponse(intent.toJson())\n                .build();\n    }\n\n    private PaymentResponse buildRefundResponse(Refund refund, PaymentStatus status) {\n        return PaymentResponse.builder()\n                .gatewayTransactionId(refund.getId())\n                .status(status)\n                .amount(new BigDecimal(refund.getAmount()).movePointLeft(refund.getCurrency().equals(\"jpy\") ? 0 : 2))\n                .currency(Currency.getInstance(refund.getCurrency().toUpperCase()))\n                .createdAt(Instant.ofEpochSecond(refund.getCreated()))\n                .rawResponse(refund.toJson())\n                .build();\n    }\n\n    private TechnicalException translateStripeException(String operation, StripeException e) {\n        if (e instanceof CardException) {\n            // Recoverable business error (declined, etc.)\n            return new BusinessException(\n                    String.format(\"Stripe card error during %s: %s\", operation, e.getMessage()), e);\n        } else if (e instanceof InvalidRequestException) {\n stripeId, referenceId);\n    }\n\n    private void auditFailure(String action, String referenceId, Exception e) {\n        auditTrail.logPaymentFailure(action, referenceId, e.getMessage());\n map.put(com.stripe.exception.InvalidRequestException.class, false);\n        map.put(com.stripe.exception.CardException.class, false);\n        return map;\n entityManager.persist(order);\n            } else {\n                order = entityManager.merge(order);\n            }\n            entityManager.flush(); // forces DB round-trip, allowing lock predicates.add(cb.equal(root.get(\"accountId\"), criteria.getAccountId()));\n        }\n\n        if (criteria.getStatus() != null) {\n            predicates.add(cb.equal(root.get(\"status\"),
